\section{Stage 14U-R: Temporal-Convergence Discretization Protocol and Refined Candidate Preflight}
\label{sec:stage14ur_temporal_convergence_protocol}

\subsection{Motivation, Governance, and Scientific Boundary}
\label{subsec:stage14ur_motivation}

While the authoritative baseline completion solve (Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02}, Package~25) advances smoothly toward terminal displacement $u = 0.0100\,\mathrm{mm}$ with enhanced cutback controls, the subsequent mandatory investigation in the Mode-I qualification sequence requires examining the sensitivity of the phase-field response to time/displacement incrementation:
\begin{quote}
\textit{Does refinement of the prescribed displacement/time-increment discretization materially alter the mechanical, spatial, or energetic response of the qualified 14,483-element adaptive mesh?}
\end{quote}
To answer this question deterministically without perturbing the active completion job, Stage~14U-R establishes a formal protocol freeze, source-identity audit, and preflight qualification for a \textbf{$2\times$ temporally refined candidate} (Package~26: \texttt{models/pandey\_kumar\_mode1/26\_stage14\_temporal\_refined\_candidate\_2x/}). Execution of the solver job on the HPC cluster is strictly withheld pending the terminal qualification of Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02}.

\subsection{Temporal Discretization Parameterization}
\label{subsec:stage14ur_parameterization}

The temporal refinement halves the initial and maximum time increment across both loading steps, corresponding to an exact factor-of-two reduction in nominal displacement increments:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Step 1 ($0.0 \to 0.0050\,\mathrm{mm}$):} The initial and maximum time increment is halved from $\Delta t_1 = 5.0\times 10^{-4}$ ($\Delta u_1 = 2.50\,\mathrm{nm}$, $2{,}000$ nominal increments) to:
    \begin{equation}
    \Delta t_{1,\mathrm{refined}} = 2.5\times 10^{-4} \implies \Delta u_{1,\mathrm{refined}} = 1.25\times 10^{-6}\,\mathrm{mm} = 1.25\,\mathrm{nm},
    \end{equation}
    with the increment ceiling increased from $\mathrm{INC}=2{,}500$ to $\mathrm{INC}=5{,}000$ ($4{,}000$ nominal increments).
    \item \textbf{Step 2 ($0.0050 \to 0.0100\,\mathrm{mm}$):} The initial and maximum time increment is halved from $\Delta t_2 = 2.0\times 10^{-4}$ ($\Delta u_2 = 1.00\,\mathrm{nm}$, $5{,}000$ nominal increments) to:
    \begin{equation}
    \Delta t_{2,\mathrm{refined}} = 1.0\times 10^{-4} \implies \Delta u_{2,\mathrm{refined}} = 0.50\times 10^{-6}\,\mathrm{mm} = 0.50\,\mathrm{nm},
    \end{equation}
    with the increment ceiling increased from $\mathrm{INC}=6{,}000$ to $\mathrm{INC}=12{,}000$ ($10{,}000$ nominal increments). The validated Stage~14U time incrementation controls:
    \begin{equation}
    \texttt{*CONTROLS, PARAMETERS=TIME INCREMENTATION: 4, 10, 9, 20, 10, 4, 0, 10}
    \end{equation}
    are strictly preserved to guarantee Newton cutback latitude down to $\Delta t_{\min} = 1.0\times 10^{-9}$.
\end{enumerate}

In Stage~14, this was upgraded with \texttt{CALL GETOUTDIR(OUTDIR\_STR, L\_OUTDIR)} and dynamic string trimming to guarantee safe telemetry file routing when executed inside HPC scratch directories. Package~26 strictly adopts the governed Stage-14 production source.
    \item \textbf{Physical and Mesh Invariance:} Complete topological and material invariance is preserved across all $14{,}456$ nodes, $14{,}483$ underlying finite elements ($43{,}449$ layered elements), material constants ($E = 210.0\,\mathrm{kN/mm}^2, \nu = 0.3, G_c = 0.0027\,\mathrm{kN/mm}, l_0 = 0.0075\,\mathrm{mm}, k = 10^{-7}$), and the verified ABI parameter order \texttt{(0.0075, 0.0027, 210.0, 0.3, 1.0e-7, 14483.0)}.

\subsection{Pre-Declared Multi-Quantity Comparison Protocol and Canonical \texorpdfstring{$K_0$}{K0} Rule}
\label{subsec:stage14ur_comparison_protocol}

To maintain rigorous scientific discipline and eliminate post-hoc evaluation bias, the multi-quantity comparison protocol is pre-declared:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Canonical Initial Stiffness ($K_0$) Rule:} To ensure consistent statistical degrees of freedom and eliminate sampling density artifacts, the $2\times$ refined response is \textbf{sampled / interpolated onto the canonical 400-point displacement grid} ($Delta u = 2.50\times 10^{-6}\,\mathrm{mm}, 0.5\Delta u < u \le 0.0010 + 0.5\Delta u, N = 400$) using the certified OLS implementation, reproducing the reference baseline anchor:
    \begin{equation}
    K_{0,\mathrm{ref}} = 137.945520\,\mathrm{kN/mm} \quad (\text{intercept } 4.472368\times 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{kN}, \; R^2 = 0.99999960).
    \end{equation}
    \item \textbf{Peak Force ($F_{\max}$) and Peak Displacement ($u_{\mathrm{peak}}$):} Maximum reaction force and corresponding prescribed displacement evaluated across the full deformation history.
    \item \textbf{10 Matched Displacement States:} Reaction force, spatial damage, and energies evaluated at $u \in \{0.0010, 0.0030, 0.0050, 0.005857, 0.0060, 0.0065, 0.0070, 0.0080, 0.0090, 0.0100\}\,\mathrm{mm}$.
    \item \textbf{Continuous Spatial Ligament Damage ($d(x, y=0.5\,\mathrm{mm})$):} Evaluated on a $1001$-point uniform grid ($x \in [0.50, 1.00]\,\mathrm{mm}, \Delta x = 0.5\,\mu\mathrm{m}$), computing continuous relative $L_2$ error and localized peak discrepancy $L_\infty$.
    \item \textbf{Macro-Crack Tip Position ($x_{\mathrm{tip}}$):} Identified via governed threshold $d \ge 0.90$.
    \item \textbf{Energy Balance Integrals:} External work $W_{\mathrm{ext}}$, elastic strain energy $E_{\mathrm{elas}}$, crack-surface functional $E_{\mathrm{frac}}$, residual $\Delta_{\mathrm{book}}$, and relative residual $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{book}}$.
    \item \textbf{Descriptive Classification Discipline:} Newly invented arbitrary percentage thresholds are rejected in favor of descriptive physical classifications: \texttt{STABLE} (variations within discretization truncation and roundoff limits), \texttt{TEMPORALLY\_SENSITIVE} (meaningful physical sensitivity to incrementation), or \texttt{NOT\_YET\_QUALIFIED} (pending completion).
\end{itemize}

\section{Stage 14U-T: Spatial-Convergence Evidence Audit and Multi-Discretization Synthesis}
\label{sec:stage14ut_spatial_convergence_audit}

\subsection{Scope, Motivation, and Methodological Distinctions}
\label{subsec:stage14ut_motivation}

Following the temporal discretization protocol qualification in Stage~14U-R/S, a rigorous investigation of spatial discretization sensitivity is required to establish the spatial convergence characteristics of the Phase-Field Model under Mode-I loading. To ensure formal academic rigor in the Master's thesis, a strict methodological distinction is enforced between two distinct evaluation concepts:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Reference-versus-Adaptive Parity (Representation Efficiency):} A single-case comparative assessment between the structured uniform reference baseline ($S_1$, $15,192$ elements, $h = 3.0\,\mu\mathrm{m} = 0.40\,l_0$) and the project target-like adaptive candidate ($14,483$ underlying elements, $h_{\min} \approx 1.09\,\mu\mathrm{m} = 0.145\,l_0$). This evaluation determines whether non-uniform spatial element concentration reproduces global structural compliance, peak capacity, and fracture dissipation at a reduced computational degree-of-freedom footprint.
    \item \textbf{True Spatial Convergence (Discretization Independence):} A systematic multi-mesh spatial discretization study across a series of progressively refined discretizations ($h/l_0$ decreasing from $0.400$ to $0.133$) with material constants, boundary conditions, user subroutine formulation, and temporal incrementation frozen. This evaluation determines whether physical response quantities approach asymptotic grid independence as $h \to 0$.
\end{enumerate}

Stage~14U-T executes a comprehensive evidence audit across all available Mode-I spatial discretizations to synthesize multi-quantity convergence metrics and assess whether additional HPC solver candidates are required.

\subsection{Comprehensive Spatial Discretization Inventory and Equivalence Matrix}
\label{subsec:stage14ut_inventory}

Table~\ref{tab:stage14ut_spatial_inventory} compiles the complete inventory of primary Mode-I spatial discretizations evaluated under the governed Gate-6B benchmark. All cases classified as \textbf{\texttt{SPATIALLY\_COMPARABLE}} share strict mathematical formulation and physical invariance:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Geometry and Seam Topology:} Domain $\Omega = 1.0\times 1.0\,\mathrm{mm}$, initial sharp crack $a_0 = 0.50\,\mathrm{mm}$ modeled as a zero-gap seam along the symmetry line $y = 0.50\,\mathrm{mm}$ with duplicated coincident node pairs to eliminate spurious notch compliance artifacts.
    \item \textbf{Boundary Conditions and Kinematics:} Bottom roller ($u_y = 0$ on $y=0$), pinned origin ($x=0, y=0: u_x=0$), top Reference Point displacement control ($u_y = u(t)$).
    \item \textbf{Material and Phase-Field Constants:} $E = 210.0\,\mathrm{kN/mm^2}$ ($210\,\mathrm{GPa}$), $\nu = 0.30$, $G_c = 0.0027\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$, $l_0 = 0.0075\,\mathrm{mm}$, and residual stiffness factor $k = 1.0\times 10^{-7}$.
    \item \textbf{Production User Subroutine:} Governed Fortran source \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for} (SHA-256 \texttt{CE8D5EDCD2911DCB018BB15275271F874E7EA62B8FB48CF4A8297469A83ACDD6}, $29{,}722$ bytes, $908$ lines) with verified 6-slot parameter ABI card \texttt{(0.0075, 0.0027, 210.0, 0.3, 1.0e-7, N\_base)}.
    \item \textbf{Companion Output Layer:} Uncoupled isotropic linear-elastic facsimile layer (\texttt{CPE4}/\texttt{CPE3}, \texttt{depvar=18}).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Crack-Corridor Mesh Statistics and Spatial Concentration}
\label{subsec:stage14ut_corridor_statistics}

A key advantage of error-guided adaptive remeshing is its ability to allocate degrees of freedom selectively into regions of high gradient while preserving coarse sizing elsewhere. To quantify this effect, the geometric properties of all finite elements within the primary fracture corridor ($y \in [0.450, 0.550]\,\mathrm{mm}, x \in [0.500, 1.000]\,\mathrm{mm}$, representing $5.0\%$ of the total specimen area) were evaluated:
\begin{itemize}
    \item \textbf{Fixed Reference $S_1$ ($15,192$ elements):} Contains $4,316$ corridor elements ($28.41\%$ of the mesh), with uniform sizing $h_{\mathrm{cor}} = 2.94\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h/l_0 = 0.392$). Far-field elements reach $h_{\max} = 20.16\,\mu\mathrm{m}$.
    \item \textbf{Fixed Intermediate $S_2$ ($32,130$ elements):} Contains $12,550$ corridor elements ($39.06\%$ of the mesh), with uniform sizing $h_{\mathrm{cor}} = 2.00\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h/l_0 = 0.267$).
    \item \textbf{Fixed Fine $S_3$ ($41,912$ elements):} Contains $17,649$ corridor elements ($42.11\%$ of the mesh), with median corridor sizing $h_{\mathrm{cor}} = 1.47\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h/l_0 = 0.196$) and $h_{\min} = 1.20\,\mu\mathrm{m}$.
    \item \textbf{Stage 14 Target-Like Adaptive Candidate ($14,483$ elements):} Concentrates $\mathbf{8,338}$ \textbf{elements} (\textbf{57.57\%} of the entire domain discretization) inside the narrow $5.0\%$ corridor area. Median corridor element size is $h_{\mathrm{median}} = 2.06\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h/l_0 = 0.274$), with near-notch elements refined down to $h_{\min} = 0.76\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h_{\min}/l_0 = 0.101$).
\end{itemize}
This spatial concentration explains why the $14,483$-element adaptive candidate achieves local notch-root resolution superior to the $41,912$-element fixed mesh while reducing total model size by $65.4\%$.

\subsection{Multi-Quantity Spatial Convergence Synthesis}
\label{subsec:stage14ut_synthesis}

An integrated analysis across the 7 spatially comparable cases yields conclusive multi-quantity findings:

\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Initial Elastic Structural Stiffness $K_0$ (\texttt{STABLE}):} Across all $7$ comparable meshes, $K_0$ varies within the narrow range $[137.823, 137.946]\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$ (Table~\ref{tab:stage14ut_spatial_inventory}). The maximum domain-wide variation is strictly $\le 0.0886\%$ (less than $0.09\%$, $R^2 \ge 0.999999$). The Stage~14 adaptive candidate ($K_0 = 137.9096\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$, $-0.0261\%$) matches the reference anchor with sub-$0.03\%$ accuracy.
    \item \textbf{Peak Reaction Force $F_{\max}$ (\texttt{MESH\_SENSITIVE}):} On the fixed-mesh sequence ($S_1 \to S_5$), peak force decreases monotonically from $0.7578\,\mathrm{kN}$ ($S_1$) to $0.7255\,\mathrm{kN}$ ($S_5$), representing an asymptotic shift of $-4.26\%$. This reduction is physically expected: finer crack-tip elements resolve the high stress gradient earlier, initiating micro-damage localization at slightly lower macroscopic load. The Stage~14 adaptive candidate ($F_{\max} = 0.7437\,\mathrm{kN}$, $-1.86\%$) lies squarely within the $S_1 \to S_2$ transition band, precisely matching its corridor median resolution ($h/l_0 = 0.274$).
    \item \textbf{Displacement at Peak $u_{\mathrm{peak}}$ (\texttt{MESH\_SENSITIVE}):} Peak displacement shifts monotonically from $5.857\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($S_1$) to $5.575\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($S_5$), a shift of $-4.81\%$. The adaptive candidate peaks at $u = 5.733\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($-2.12\%$, matching the fixed-mesh trajectory).
    \item \textbf{Post-Fracture Fracture Functional $E_{\mathrm{frac}}$ (\texttt{STABLE}):} Across all discretizations ($h/l_0 \in [0.400, 0.133]$), the total dissipated crack-surface energy functional in the broken state converges within $[2.285, 2.375]\,\mathrm{mJ}$ (an overall min-max spread of $< 3.9\%$). The Stage~14 adaptive candidate yields $E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 2.2855\,\mathrm{mJ}$ ($-2.34\%$ vs $S_1$), confirming thermodynamic consistency.
    \item \textbf{Crack Path and Localization Front Morphology (\texttt{STABLE}):} All comparable simulations produce a strictly straight horizontal crack path along the symmetry line $y = 0.500\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($|\Delta y|_{\max} = 0.000\,\mathrm{mm}$), with regularized localization front width $w_{90} \approx 2.46\text{--}2.62\,l_0$.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Scientific Decision and Governance Verdict}
\label{subsec:stage14ut_verdict}

With 7 spatially comparable simulations spanning 5 fixed structured meshes and 2 native adaptive meshes covering model sizes from $13.9\mathrm{k}$ to $69.4\mathrm{k}$ elements, the spatial convergence continuum of the Mode-I benchmark is complete and thoroughly qualified. No additional spatial solver jobs are needed for the thesis documentation.

The formal verdict of Stage~14U-T is recorded as:
\begin{center}
{\footnotesize\textbf{\texttt{SPATIAL\_CONVERGENCE\_EVIDENCE\_ALREADY\_SUFFICIENT}}}.
\end{center}

\subsection{Geometric Proof of Crack-Corridor Area and Refinement Concentration}
\label{subsec:stage14uu_geometry_proof}

The crack propagation corridor is formally defined as:
\begin{equation}
\Omega_{\mathrm{corridor}} = \left\{ (x, y) \in [0.500, 1.000]\,\mathrm{mm} \times [0.450, 0.550]\,\mathrm{mm} \right\}.
\end{equation}
The total area of the corridor and the full specimen domain $\Omega = [0, 1] \times [0, 1]\,\mathrm{mm}$ are:
\begin{equation}
\mathrm{Area}(\Omega_{\mathrm{corridor}}) = 0.500\,\mathrm{mm} \times 0.100\,\mathrm{mm} = 0.050\,\mathrm{mm}^2, \quad \mathrm{Area}(\Omega) = 1.000\,\mathrm{mm}^2,
\end{equation}
yielding an exact geometric area fraction of:
\begin{equation}
\frac{\mathrm{Area}(\Omega_{\mathrm{corridor}})}{\mathrm{Area}(\Omega)} = \frac{0.050}{1.000} = 5.00\%.
\end{equation}
Evaluating element centroids against this domain boundary proves that the Stage-14 adaptive mesh concentrates $\mathbf{57.57\%}$ ($8{,}338$ elements) of its total discretization into this $5\%$ corridor area. In contrast, the fixed reference mesh $S_1$ contains only $28.41\%$ ($4{,}316$ elements) in the corridor, while the Package~24 adaptive mesh allocates $12.20\%$ ($1{,}696$ elements). This geometric concentration explains the superior notch-root resolution ($h_{\min} = 0.76\,\mu\mathrm{m} = 0.101\,l_0$) of Stage~14 at low overall degrees of freedom.

\subsection{Three-Tier Claim Separation}
\label{subsec:stage14uu_three_tier_claims}

To preserve epistemological clarity, three distinct categories of spatial claims are separated:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Tier 1: Reference-Adaptive Agreement (Representation Efficiency Parity):} Certified \texttt{STABLE}. The Stage-14 adaptive candidate reproduces the linear-elastic stiffness ($K_0 = 137.9096\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$, $-0.0261\%$ vs $S_1$), peak load ($F_{\max} = 0.7437\,\mathrm{kN}$, $-1.86\%$ vs $S_1$, situated in the $S_1 \to S_2$ transition), and broken-state fracture energy ($E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 2.2855\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $-2.34\%$ vs $S_1$) with high representation efficiency.
    \item \textbf{Tier 2: Fixed-Mesh Spatial Sensitivity (Asymptotic Grid Independence):} Certified \texttt{STABLE} for elastic stiffness ($K_0$ variation $\le 0.0886\%$) and broken-state dissipation ($E_{\mathrm{frac}} \in [2.285, 2.375]\,\mathrm{mJ}$, spread $< 3.9\%$), and \texttt{MESH\_SENSITIVE} for peak capacity ($F_{\max}$ decreases monotonically $0.758 \to 0.725\,\mathrm{kN}$, $-4.26\%$ from $S_1 \to S_5$) and displacement ($u_{\mathrm{peak}}$ shifts $5.86 \to 5.58\,\mu\mathrm{m}$).
    \item \textbf{Tier 3: Adaptive Spatial Sensitivity (Refinement Methodology Invariance):} Certified \texttt{STABLE}. Comparison between Package~24 and Package~25 confirms that both adaptive formulations yield near-identical macroscopic fracture responses ($F_{\max}$ within $0.05\%$, $E_{\mathrm{frac}}$ within $0.20\%$, straight crack path $y=0.500\,\mathrm{mm}$), with Package~25 offering localized notch-root refinement ($4.66\times$ corridor density ratio).
\end{enumerate}

\section{Stage 14U-W: Energy-Claims Discipline, Matched-State Consistency, and Thermodynamic-Interpretation Audit}
\label{sec:stage14uw_energy_claims_discipline}

\subsection{Epistemic Governance and Thermodynamic-Interpretation Discipline}
\label{subsec:stage14uw_motivation}

Prior to closing the Mode-I benchmark validation baseline, Stage~14U-W executes an exhaustive energy-claims discipline, matched-state consistency, and thermodynamic-interpretation correction audit across the complete Mode-I simulation archive. In strict adherence to project epistemic governance (\textit{"We need to have understood everything related to the first model before we increase complexity"}), this audit enforces three foundational corrections:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{State Functional vs.\ Dissipation Distinction:} The regularized phase-field fracture energy term $E_{\mathrm{frac}}(d) = \int_\Omega G_c \left[ \frac{d^2}{2l_0} + \frac{l_0}{2} |\nabla d|^2 \right] \mathrm{d}\Omega$ implemented in the authoritative user subroutine \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for} (SHA-256: \texttt{CE8D5EDCD2911DCB018BB15275271F874E7EA62B8FB48CF4A8297469A83ACDD6}) is the \textbf{Bourdin--Francfort--Marigo regularized crack surface energy state functional $\Gamma_l(d)$}. It represents an instantaneous conservative spatial functional of the state $d(\mathbf{x})$, \textbf{not cumulative irreversible thermodynamic dissipation} $\int \dot{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathrm{frac}}\,\mathrm{d}t$. All prior phrasing attributing empirical proofs of thermodynamic conservation or equating $E_{\mathrm{frac}}$ with cumulative dissipation is formally withdrawn.
    \item \textbf{Numerical Bookkeeping Residual Discipline:} The quantity $\Delta_{\mathrm{book}} \equiv W_{\mathrm{ext}} - (E_{\mathrm{elas}} + E_{\mathrm{frac}})$ is a \textbf{numerical bookkeeping residual} arising from discrete time-increment trapezoidal integration, spatial finite element discretization, and penalty parameter stiffness residual ($k = 10^{-7}$). It serves as an internal numerical consistency diagnostic, not an empirical thermodynamic proof.
    \item \textbf{Censored Endpoints and Matched-State Comparison:} Simulations across distinct spatial, temporal, and regularizing discretizations terminate at different macroscopic displacements ($u_{\mathrm{term}} = 0.0058$ to $0.0100\,\mathrm{mm}$) due to either completed loading or solver cutback limits. Consequently, comparisons must not group unmatched terminal states into an undifferentiated "broken-state spread"; rather, energy partitioning must be evaluated at strictly matched displacement horizons ($u = 1.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$, $3.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$, $5.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$, and $6.5\,\mu\mathrm{m}$).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Exact Implemented Energy Identities in Subroutine \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for}}
\label{subsec:stage14uw_identities}

\section{Stage 14U-Y: Adaptive Spatial-Field and Energy Sensitivity Closure Audit}
\label{sec:stage14uy_adaptive_spatial_energy_audit}

\subsection{Motivation and Resolution of the Matched-State Energy Discrepancy}
\label{subsec:stage14uy_discrepancy_resolution}

A primary question arising from multi-model adaptive benchmarking in Gate~6B concerns the energetic and spatial response across different adaptive discretizations at comparable total element counts. Specifically, an apparent discrepancy was noted between the preliminary 2\% uniform-error adaptive model (Package~24, Job~\texttt{1409846.mmaster02}, $N_{\mathrm{base}} = 13{,}897$ finite elements) and the Stage~14 reference-fidelity adaptive model (Package~25, Job~\texttt{1409953.mmaster02}, $N_{\mathrm{base}} = 14{,}483$ finite elements). At displacement $u = 0.0065\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($6.5\,\mu\mathrm{m}$), Package~24 was reported with an energy of $2.6874\,\mathrm{mJ}$, whereas Stage~14 produced $E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 2.2835\,\mathrm{mJ}$ (matching the structured $S_1$ reference $2.3390\,\mathrm{mJ}$ within $-2.26\%$), despite near-identical initial stiffness ($K_0$ within $0.014\%$) and peak reaction force ($F_{\max}$ within $0.054\%$).

Exhaustive accounting of the constitutive state variables in subroutine \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for} resolves this discrepancy:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Total Energy vs.\ Fracture Functional Discrepancy:} The $2.6874\,\mathrm{mJ}$ value recorded for Package~24 at $u = 0.0065\,\mathrm{mm}$ represents \emph{Total Stored Energy} $E_{\mathrm{total}} = E_{\mathrm{elas}} + E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 1.9033\,\mathrm{mJ} + 0.7842\,\mathrm{mJ} = 2.6874\,\mathrm{mJ}$. At this intermediate displacement, the crack in Package~24 has not finished propagating through the ligament, leaving the upper and lower halves under significant elastic tension ($E_{\mathrm{elas}} = 1.9033\,\mathrm{mJ}$).
  \item \textbf{Stage 14 Severed State Parity:} In Stage~14, crack propagation snaps through cleanly between $u = 5.73\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ and $u = 6.00\,\mu\mathrm{m}$. By $u = 0.0065\,\mathrm{mm}$, the ligament is fully severed ($x_{\mathrm{tip}} = 0.9985\,\mathrm{mm}$), the elastic strain energy has unloaded to $E_{\mathrm{elas}} = 0.0067\,\mathrm{mJ}$, and the crack-surface functional has stabilized at $E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 2.2835\,\mathrm{mJ}$ ($E_{\mathrm{total}} = 2.2902\,\mathrm{mJ}$).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Spatial Mesh Concentration, Localization Width, and Numerical Bridging}
\label{subsec:stage14uy_mesh_concentration}

The physical cause of the divergent post-peak propagation dynamics lies in the spatial element concentration inside the $5.0\%$ geometric crack corridor $\Omega_{\mathrm{corridor}} = [0.5, 1.0] \times [0.45, 0.55]\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($0.0500\,\mathrm{mm}^2$):
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Package 24 (Global Refinement):} Driven by a global 2\% error indicator tolerance, Package~24 distributed elements broadly across the bulk, placing only $12.20\%$ of its elements ($1{,}696$ elements) inside the corridor. Outside the immediate notch root, the mesh coarsens to $h \approx 2.58\text{--}5.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h/l_0 \approx 0.35\text{--}0.67$). When the crack advances into this coarser zone, the discrete element size is unable to resolve the steep gradient $|\nabla d| \sim 1/l_0$, causing the damage band to widen artificially ($w_{d \ge 0.5}$ increases). This numerical bridging delays crack breakthrough, sustaining high elastic loads until $u \approx 0.0090\,\mathrm{mm}$, where the fully broken state overestimates fracture energy ($E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 3.1926\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $+36.4\%$ above $S_1$).
  \item \textbf{Stage 14 (Targeted Corridor Refinement):} In Stage~14, $57.49\%$ of elements ($8{,}326$ elements, a $4.91\times$ higher concentration) reside inside the corridor, providing $h_{\min} = 0.760\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h/l_0 = 0.101$) and median corridor sizing $h = 2.058\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ ($h/l_0 = 0.274$). The regularized phase-field profile is sharply resolved along the entire propagation path, avoiding numerical bridging and capturing rapid unloading at $u = 0.0058\text{--}0.0060\,\mathrm{mm}$.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Methodological Classification and Governance Verdict}
\label{subsec:stage14uy_governance_verdict}

From an epistemological and numerical perspective, Package~24 and Stage~14 represent **two distinct adaptive-refinement distribution strategies at similar total element budgets (~14k elements)**, **NOT a conventional $h$-refinement convergence pair**:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Macroscopic Invariance:} Both distributions yield identical structural stiffness ($K_0 = 137.89\text{--}137.91\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$, diff $0.014\%$) and peak load ($F_{\max} = 0.7423\text{--}0.7437\,\mathrm{kN}$, diff $0.054\%$), demonstrating that pre-peak structural behavior is governed solely by the fine notch-root mesh and is insensitive to far-field element distribution.
  \item \textbf{Field Sensitivity in Softening:} Dynamic crack propagation across the ligament requires fine resolution ($h/l_0 \le 0.27$) along the full crack path. Insufficient corridor refinement (Package~24) broadens the localized band, producing delayed breakthrough and artificial dissipation inflation.
\end{enumerate}

Based on this evidence, the governing classifications are formally assigned:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Methodological Classification:} \texttt{ADAPTIVE\_SPATIAL\_COMPARISON\_NOT\_A\_CONVERGENCE\_PAIR}
  \item \textbf{Adaptive Macro vs.\ Field Verdict:} \texttt{ADAPTIVE\_MACRO\_RESPONSE\_STABLE\_\_PHASE\_FIELD\_MESH\_SENSITIVE}
  \item \textbf{Overall Spatial Convergence Status:} \texttt{SPATIAL\_CONVERGENCE\_EVIDENCE\_ALREADY\_SUFFICIENT}
\end{itemize}

\section{Phase-Field Regularization Length-Scale Sensitivity, Matched-State Boundary Audit, and Resolution-Adequacy Qualification (Stage 14U-AA)}
\label{sec:stage14uaa_length_scale_sensitivity}

\subsection{Continuum Nature of $l_0$, 1D Scaling Background, and Resolution Discipline}
\label{subsec:stage14uaa_l0_nature}

In regularized variational phase-field fracture mechanics, the length-scale parameter $l_0$ is designated strictly as the \textbf{phase-field regularization/internal length parameter of the continuum model}, and \textbf{not a numerical discretization parameter}. It governs the spatial regularization of the crack-surface topology and defines the implemented regularized crack-surface functional in the AT2 formulation:
\begin{equation}
  \Gamma_{l_0}(d) = \int_\Omega \left( \frac{1}{2 l_0} d^2 + \frac{l_0}{2} |\nabla d|^2 \right) \mathrm{d}\Omega
  \label{eq:bourdin_gamma_functional_l0}
\end{equation}
In contrast to finite-element mesh refinement ($h \to 0$ with $l_0$ held constant), altering $l_0$ modifies the underlying continuum governing equations and physical process-zone width. Therefore, length-scale variations test \textbf{model-parameter sensitivity}, whereas mesh-refinement studies assess \textbf{numerical discretization convergence}.

\paragraph{Idealized 1D AT2 Theoretical Scaling Background.}
In a one-dimensional homogeneous bar under monotonic tension, the analytical critical stress for damage initiation in the AT2 phase-field formulation is:
\begin{equation}
  \sigma_c = \sqrt{\frac{9 E G_c}{16 l_0}}
  \label{eq:at2_1d_critical_stress}
\end{equation}
This relation serves strictly as \textbf{idealized 1D theoretical background} for the inverse square-root scaling $\sigma_c \propto 1/\sqrt{l_0}$. In two-dimensional notched configurations with severe non-uniform stress concentration at the notch root, Equation~\eqref{eq:at2_1d_critical_stress} provides qualitative scaling context rather than proof of numerical finite-element solution correctness.

\paragraph{Descriptive Mesh Resolution Reporting.}
Published literature commonly recommends mesh resolution ratios $h_{\min}/l_0 \le 0.20\text{--}0.50$ (e.g., Miehe et al., Borden et al., Molnar et al.) to ensure that the regularized phase-field profile is adequately captured on the spatial grid. In the present investigation, measured resolution ratios on the structured 41,912-element mesh are reported descriptively:
\begin{itemize}
  \item $L_1$ Baseline ($l_0 = 0.00750\,\mathrm{mm}$): $h_{\min}/l_0 = 0.00150 / 0.00750 = 0.2000$ ($h_{\min} = l_0/5$);
  \item $L_2$ Intermediate ($l_0 = 0.01125\,\mathrm{mm}$): $h_{\min}/l_0 = 0.00150 / 0.01125 = 0.1333$ ($h_{\min} = l_0/7.5$);
  \item $L_3$ Coarse ($l_0 = 0.01500\,\mathrm{mm}$): $h_{\min}/l_0 = 0.00150 / 0.01500 = 0.1000$ ($h_{\min} = l_0/10$);
  \item $S_1$ Reference Anchor ($l_0 = 0.00750\,\mathrm{mm}$, $15{,}192$ elements): $h_{\min}/l_0 = 0.00300 / 0.00750 = 0.4000$ ($h_{\min} = l_0/2.5$).
\end{itemize}
These ratios are recorded descriptively as contextual guidelines. Because multi-grid spatial $h$-refinement was not independently executed for each distinct $l_0$, resolution adequacy is classified within its exact empirical boundary: \texttt{DESCRIPTIVELY\_REPORTED\_\_RESOLUTION\_ADEQUACY\_NOT\_INDEPENDENTLY\_QUALIFIED}.

\subsection{Mechanical Response Scaling and Energy Evolution}
\label{subsec:stage14uaa_mechanical_scaling}

As illustrated in Figure~\ref{fig:stage14uaa_fu_overlay} and Figure~\ref{fig:stage14uaa_scaling}, the mechanical response demonstrates clear physical scaling laws:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Initial Structural Stiffness Invariance (\texttt{STABLE}):} Across the $2.0\times$ variation in $l_0$ on the 42k-element mesh, $K_0$ decreases by only $0.107\%$ ($137.824 \to 137.676\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$). In the linear elastic regime ($u \le 1.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$), phase damage is negligible ($d < 10^{-4}$), meaning the structural compliance is governed entirely by intact elastic continuum deformation.
  \item \textbf{Monotonic Peak Load Reduction (\texttt{LENGTH\_SCALE\_SENSITIVE}):} Peak reaction force decreases monotonically from $F_{\max} = 0.7255\,\mathrm{kN}$ ($l_0 = 7.5\,\mu\mathrm{m}$) to $0.7084\,\mathrm{kN}$ ($-2.36\%$, $l_0 = 11.25\,\mu\mathrm{m}$) and $0.6895\,\mathrm{kN}$ ($-4.96\%$, $l_0 = 15.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$). In 1D AT2 phase-field theory, the critical initiation stress scales as $\sigma_c = \sqrt{9 E G_c / (16 l_0)} \propto 1/\sqrt{l_0}$. In the 2D notched square domain, the pre-existing notch stress concentration ($a_0 = 0.5\,\mathrm{mm}$) moderates the sensitivity, while preserving the monotonic load reduction.
  \item \textbf{Pre-Peak Crack-Surface Functional Evolution (\texttt{LENGTH\_SCALE\_SENSITIVE}):} Figure~\ref{fig:stage14uaa_energy_comparison}(b) demonstrates that broader regularization kernels ($l_0 = 15.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$) accumulate larger pre-peak crack-surface energy ($E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 0.0685\,\mathrm{mJ}$ at $u = 5.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$, an $+87.2\%$ increase over $l_0 = 7.5\,\mu\mathrm{m}$) because diffuse damage initiates over a larger volume around the notch root.
  \item \textbf{Post-Fracture Crack-Surface Functional Stability (\texttt{POST\_FRACTURE\_EFRAC\_STABLE\_OVER\_TESTED\_LENGTH\_SCALE\_RANGE}):} Upon complete macroscopic fracture traversal, the implemented crack-surface functional evaluates to $E_{\mathrm{frac}} \in [2.302, 2.357]\,\mathrm{mJ}$ (spread $< 2.31\%$ across all tested length scales), confirming physical crack-surface functional stability across the tested parameter window.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Completion-Control Peak-Region Parity and First-Divergence Audit (Stage 14U-AB)}
\label{sec:stage14uab_parity_audit}

\subsection{Motivation, Incrementation Parameterization, and Parity Scope}
\label{subsec:stage14uab_motivation}

Following the premature cutback exhaustion of the baseline Stage-14 adaptive solve (Job~\texttt{1409953.mmaster02}) at $u = 0.007889\,\mathrm{mm}$ in Step~2, a completion rerun was commissioned as Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02}. The completion package modified Abaqus time-incrementation controls strictly within Step~2:
\begin{equation}
  I_A = 10, \quad I_C = 20, \quad I_R = 10, \quad \Delta t_{\min} = 1.0 \times 10^{-9}
\end{equation}
compared to baseline standard parameters ($I_A=5, I_C=16, \Delta t_{\min}=1.0 \times 10^{-5}$), providing four additional orders of magnitude in cutback latitude and relaxing the severe-discontinuity convergence limits to traverse the dynamic snap-through crack-arrest zone.

A critical methodological question must be rigorously addressed before interpreting any completed trajectory: \emph{Do these modified time-incrementation controls alter the converged physical equilibrium branch, the initial elastic stiffness, the crack initiation point, or the peak reaction force prior to the cutback failure boundary?}

To answer this question conclusively, Stage~14U-AB conducts an increment-by-increment parity audit between predecessor Job~\texttt{1409953.mmaster02} and active Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02} across the entire common reached displacement range ($u \in [0, 0.006198]\,\mathrm{mm}$, comprising 3,198 increments).

\subsection{Bitwise Discrepancy Analysis Across Reached Trajectory}
\label{subsec:stage14uab_discrepancy}

The comparison evaluates all 2,000 increments of Step~1 ($u \in [0, 0.0050]\,\mathrm{mm}$) and the first 1,198 increments of Step~2 ($u \in (0.0050, 0.006198]\,\mathrm{mm}$). For every increment $i$, the displacement delta $|\Delta u_i| = |u_{i,\mathrm{act}} - u_{i,\mathrm{pred}}|$, reaction force delta $|\Delta F_i| = |F_{i,\mathrm{act}} - F_{i,\mathrm{pred}}|$, relative force error $|\Delta F_i| / F_i$, and energetic deltas ($|\Delta W_{\mathrm{ext}}|$, $|\Delta E_{\mathrm{elas}}|$, $|\Delta E_{\mathrm{frac}}|$) were evaluated.

\subsection{Canonical Initial Structural Stiffness and Peak Load Parity}
\label{subsec:stage14uab_peak_parity}

The canonical initial structural stiffness was calculated using the pre-declared 400-point ordinary least-squares regression protocol over $u \in [0, 0.0010]\,\mathrm{mm}$. Both runs yield bitwise identical regression metrics:
\begin{equation}
  K_0 = 137.909558\,\mathrm{kN/mm}, \quad R^2 = 0.99999960, \quad C = 4.4795 \times 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{kN}
\end{equation}
representing a negligible $-0.0261\%$ deviation from the uniform fine reference anchor ($K_{0,\mathrm{ref}} = 137.945520\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$), confirming the classification \texttt{STABLE}.

Both models track the identical equilibrium path through linear elasticity, crack initiation, and the adaptive peak ($F_{\max} = 0.7437\,\mathrm{kN}$).}
  \label{fig:stage14uab_parity_overlay}
\end{figure}

In Step~2, crack initiation and macroscopic peak force are achieved at Step~2 Increment~733 ($u = 0.005733\,\mathrm{mm}$):
\begin{itemize}
  \item Predecessor Run~\texttt{1409953}: $F_{\max} = 0.74370080\,\mathrm{kN}$, $W_{\mathrm{ext}} = 2.207314\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $E_{\mathrm{elas}} = 2.131815\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 0.075642\,\mathrm{mJ}$;
  \item Active Completion Run~\texttt{1409982}: $F_{\max} = 0.74370082\,\mathrm{kN}$, $W_{\mathrm{ext}} = 2.207314\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $E_{\mathrm{elas}} = 2.131815\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $E_{\mathrm{frac}} = 0.075642\,\mathrm{mJ}$.
\end{itemize}
The peak reaction force difference is $|\Delta F_{\max}| = 2.0 \times 10^{-8}\,\mathrm{kN}$ ($0.000003\%$), and the energy bookkeeping residual at peak evaluates to $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{book}} = 0.006445\%$ in both models.

\paragraph{Solver Control Non-Invasiveness and Causal Attribution.}
This mathematical equivalence confirms an essential scientific distinction: The adaptive-vs-fixed peak offset is not caused by the Stage-14U solver-control modification over the verified common solution range. Its remaining origin is associated with differences between the adaptive and fixed discretizations/formulations and must not be attributed more narrowly without direct evidence.

\section{Native Adaptive Remesh Provenance Closure, ODB Field Equivalence, and Causal Discipline (Stage 14U-AC)}
\label{sec:stage14uac_native_remesh_provenance}

To establish complete reproducibility ahead of external evaluation, Stage~14U-AC closes the provenance chain of the 14,483-element adaptive discretization (\texttt{PK\_MODE1\_STAGE14\_ADAPT\_14K\_FRACTURE.inp}), audits the content-level equivalence of historical output databases, verifies native mesh topology and three-layer reconstruction, and enforces strict causal discipline regarding structural response differences.

\subsection{Pre-Analysis Source and Companion Continuum Formulation}
\label{subsec:stage14uac_preanalysis_source}

The spatial refinement pattern for the 14,483-element mesh was generated by a companion pre-analysis executed on the Freiberg high-performance computing cluster:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Pre-Analysis Source Job:} Interactive single-CPU execution \texttt{INTERACTIVE\_93} on node \texttt{mnode097};
  \item \textbf{Input Deck:} \texttt{PK\_M1\_JOB1\_INF\_COMPANION\_2906.inp} (Table~\ref{tab:stage14uac_provenance_matrix});
  \item \textbf{Companion Subroutine:} \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel\_inf\_stress.for};
  \item \textbf{Discretization Base:} Coarse companion mesh of 2,906 continuum plane-strain elements (2,818 CPE4 and 88 CPE3 elements);
  \item \textbf{Output Requests:} Element set \texttt{All\_elem} requesting \texttt{MISESERI}, \texttt{MISESAVG}, $\sten$, and $\eten$ at whole-element positions with frequency~1 across both steps.
\end{itemize}
In this companion run, \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel\_inf\_stress.for} evaluates linear-elastic stress states without damage degradation, providing the stress field from which Abaqus/CAE computes the elemental energy-norm error indicator $\eta_e = \|e_\sigma\|_{L_2} / \|\sigma\|_{L_2}$ via patch stress recovery.

\subsection{ODB Provenance and Content-Level Field Equivalence Audit}
\label{subsec:stage14uac_odb_audit}

Two physical instances of the companion output database exist in the project records:

The raw binary files differ by exactly 32 bytes due to operating system build timestamps and endian header padding in the underlying Abaqus HDF5/ODB container format. Because the cryptographic hashes differ (Table~\ref{tab:stage14uac_provenance_matrix}), these files are classified as distinct binary containers (\texttt{DIFFERING\_RAW\_SHA256\_CONTAINERS}) and cannot be declared binary aliases.

To verify whether this container difference affects remeshing results, a point-by-point field audit was performed across all nodes, elements, frames, and \texttt{MISESERI} fields:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Nodal Coordinates:} Exactly 2,989 nodes; coordinate hashes are bitwise identical (\texttt{088e5076d5bfdb5a});
  \item \textbf{Element Connectivity:} Exactly 8,718 total elements (2,818 CPE4, 88 CPE3, and 5,812 internal reference nodes); connectivity hashes are bitwise identical (\texttt{fa47e80b8aa23210});
  \item \textbf{Step/Frame Structure:} Exactly 501 frames in Step~1 and 1,022 frames in Step~2;
  \item \textbf{Element-Wise Error Indicator Parity:}
    \begin{itemize}
      \item Step~1 final increment ($u = 0.0050\,\mathrm{mm}$): across all 2,906 base elements, $\max|\Delta\eta_e| = 0.0$ (bitwise identical);
      \item Step~2 Frame~880 ($u = 0.0094\,\mathrm{mm}$): across all 2,906 base elements, $\max|\Delta\eta_e| = 0.0$ (bitwise identical);
      \item Step~2 final increment ($u = 0.0100\,\mathrm{mm}$): across all 2,906 base elements, $\max|\Delta\eta_e| = 3.31 \times 10^{-24}$, with zero elements exhibiting $|\Delta\eta_e| > 10^{-18}$.
    \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}
Because all remeshing-relevant field outputs are identical within numerical machine precision, the ODB relationship is formally certified:
\begin{center}
  \texttt{STAGE14\_NATIVE\_REMESH\_PROVENANCE\_CLOSED\_BY\_FIELD\_EQUIVALENCE}
\end{center}

Nodal coordinates match the native mesh with $0.00\,\mathrm{mm}$ discrepancy across all 14,456 part nodes. Element connectivity is identical across all three layers ($14{,}483/14{,}483$, $100\%$) and forms a strict 1-to-1 canonical bijection to the native mesh elements.

\subsection{Epistemic Classification and Causal Attribution Boundary}
\label{subsec:stage14uac_epistemic_causal}

To ensure complete clarity regarding the sources of scientific evidence, the components of the Stage-14 provenance chain are categorized into three epistemic tiers:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Source-Verified:} Input decks, user subroutine implementations, Abaqus/CAE scripting parameters, and output mesh decks archived in the project repository with verifiable cryptographic hashes;
  \item \textbf{Numerically-Verified:} Empirical results verified by direct calculation, including ODB content-level field equivalence ($\Delta\eta_e \le 3.31 \times 10^{-24}$), mesh non-inversion, slit topology preservation, and 100\% three-layer bijective mapping;
  \item \textbf{Unresolved Internal Detail:} Proprietary Abaqus/CAE algorithms governing patch recovery and quad-dominant element generation. While the error indicator is based on Zienkiewicz--Zhu recovery principles, the exact internal smoothing, transition grading, and sizing equations are proprietary to SIMULIA. In this thesis, the sizing relationship is characterized as the adopted thesis formulation rather than asserting private knowledge of proprietary solver source code.
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Disciplined Causal Attribution.}
In earlier analyses, the peak reaction force offset between the adaptive candidate and the uniform fixed reference ($F_{\max} = 0.7437\,\mathrm{kN}$ vs $0.7578\,\mathrm{kN}$, a $-1.86\%$ difference) was preliminarily attributed exclusively to spatial discretization. Under Stage~14U-AC, this causal claim is formally disciplined:
\begin{quote}
  \itshape
  The adaptive-vs-fixed peak offset ($0.7437\,\mathrm{kN}$ vs $0.7578\,\mathrm{kN}$) is not caused by the Stage-14U solver-control modification over the verified common solution range. Its remaining origin is associated with differences between the adaptive and fixed discretizations/formulations (such as localized mesh density grading, unstructured quad/tri transitions, and companion elastic-preanalysis sizing) and must not be attributed more narrowly without direct evidence.
\end{quote}
This rigorous boundary respects the exact empirical evidence and prevents unverified causal overreach in thesis conclusions.




\section{Stage 14U-AD: 4-Thread Shared-Memory Parity Qualification and Displacement Schedule Verification}
\label{sec:stage14uad_4thread_parity}

To establish a verified baseline for accelerating future multi-mesh and parameter-scaling simulations on high-performance computing resources, Stage~14U-AD addresses two core objectives:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Displacement Schedule and Live Telemetry Verification:} Resolving an inconsistency in earlier manual displacement estimations by documenting the exact two-step loading schedule and validating against direct node output telemetry;
  \item \textbf{4-Thread Shared-Memory Stage-A Parity Qualification:} Systematically qualifying a 4-thread shared-memory execution candidate (\texttt{PK\_M1\_14K\_4T}, Job ID \texttt{1410006.mmaster02}) against the authoritative serial 1-CPU reference (\texttt{PK\_M1\_ADAPT\_14K\_FRACTURE}, Job ID \texttt{1409982.mmaster02}).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Governed Loading Schedule and Telemetry Reconciliation}
\label{subsec:stage14uad_loading_schedule}

The authoritative solve deck \texttt{PK\_MODE1\_STAGE14\_ADAPT\_14K\_FRACTURE.inp} applies monotonic Mode-I tensile loading via a Reference Point (\texttt{N\_RP}, node 999999) coupled to the top boundary (\texttt{N\_TOP}) across two distinct analysis steps:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Step 1 (Pre-Peak Elastic Loading):} Prescribes $u_y = 0.0050\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($5.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$) over step pseudo-time $t_1 = 1.0$ using 2,000 fixed increments ($\Delta t_1 = 0.0005$):
  \begin{equation}
    u(t_1) = 0.0050 \times t_1 \quad (\text{for } 0 \le t_1 \le 1.0)
    \label{eq:stage14uad_step1_ramp}
  \end{equation}
  \item \textbf{Step 2 (Fracture Propagation and Softening):} Prescribes $u_y = 0.0100\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($10.0\,\mu\mathrm{m}$) over step pseudo-time $t_2 = 1.0$ using 5,000 initial increments ($\Delta t_2 = 0.0002$):
  \begin{equation}
    u(t_2) = 0.0050 + (0.0100 - 0.0050) \times t_2 = 0.0050 + 0.0050 \times t_2 \quad (\text{for } 0 \le t_2 \le 1.0)
    \label{eq:stage14uad_step2_ramp}
  \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Reconciliation of Earlier Manual Estimate.}
In prior live monitoring checkpoints, a manual estimation formula erroneously assumed Step~1 terminated at $u = 0.0010\,\mathrm{mm}$, resulting in an erroneous displacement value of $u \approx 0.00454\,\mathrm{mm}$ at Step~2 $t_2 = 0.3940$. Under the verified governed schedule in Eq.~\eqref{eq:stage14uad_step2_ramp}, the true physical displacement at $t_2 = 0.3940$ is:
\begin{equation}
  u = 0.0050 + 0.0050 \times 0.3940 = 0.006970\,\mathrm{mm} = 6.970\,\mu\mathrm{m}
\end{equation}
Direct inspection of Abaqus \texttt{.dat} file tables for Node 999999 confirms exact agreement with Eq.~\eqref{eq:stage14uad_step2_ramp}: at $t_2 = 0.4048$ (Increment 2,024), $U_2 = 7.0240 \times 10^{-3}\,\mathrm{mm} = 7.024\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ and reaction force $RF_2 = 2.0521 \times 10^{-3}\,\mathrm{kN}$ in the deep post-peak softening tail (Figure~\ref{fig:stage14uad_4thread_parity}(a)).

\subsection{Execution Architecture and Subroutine Thread-Safety Protocol}
\label{subsec:stage14uad_architecture}

Because the phase-field UEL formulation in \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for} relies on mutable shared common blocks (\texttt{COMMON /CB\_STATE\_TRANS/}) to exchange trial states between co-located displacement and phase elements, distributed-memory multi-rank MPI execution is strictly unqualified. However, shared-memory multi-threading within a single process is permitted subject to a rigorous two-stage qualification protocol:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Stage A (Serial-vs-Threaded Parity):} Execution of an exact 4-thread twin ($1\text{ MPI rank} \times 4\text{ threads}$) against the certified 1-CPU serial reference over identical physical states;
  \item \textbf{Stage B (Independent Determinism Repeat):} Execution of an independent repeat of the 4-thread run to prove deterministic numerical convergence.
\end{itemize}

The 4-thread candidate was deployed in package \texttt{26\_stage14\_adaptive\_candidate\_14k\_4thread} with execution command:
\begin{quote}
  \texttt{abaqus job=PK\_M1\_14K\_4T\_STAGE\_A user=f42\_mixed\_uel.for input=PK\_MODE1\_STAGE14\_ADAPT\_14K\_FRACTURE.inp cpus=4 mp\_mode=threads memory="16gb" double=both interactive}
\end{quote}
Startup telemetry inspection of the solver \texttt{.com} driver file confirmed \texttt{'cpus': 4}, \texttt{'mp\_mode': THREADS}, and single-node allocation on \texttt{mnode097/1*4}, proving zero distributed MPI rank proliferation.

\subsection{Stage-A Numerical Parity Results}
\label{subsec:stage14uad_parity_results}

A pointwise audit comparing 4-thread Job \texttt{1410006.mmaster02} and serial Job \texttt{1409982.mmaster02} across all common reached increments established:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Reaction Force Parity:} Maximum absolute discrepancy $|\Delta F|_{\max} = 0.00000000\,\mathrm{kN}$ (bitwise identical);
  \item \textbf{Elastic Strain Energy Parity:} Maximum absolute discrepancy $|\Delta E_{\text{elas}}|_{\max} = 0.000000\,\mathrm{mJ}$ (bitwise identical);
  \item \textbf{Fracture Functional Parity:} Maximum absolute discrepancy $|\Delta E_{\text{frac}}|_{\max} = 0.000000\,\mathrm{mJ}$ (bitwise identical);
  \item \textbf{Solver Convergence Health:} Exactly 3 Newton--Raphson equilibrium iterations per increment with zero cutbacks.
\end{enumerate}

Based on these verified metrics, the 4-thread execution candidate is assigned the formal status \texttt{THREAD\_PARITY\_PASS\_OVER\_REACHED\_RANGE}. The serial run \texttt{1409982.mmaster02} continues solving undisturbed as the authoritative 1-CPU completion reference.


\section{Stage 14U-AE: 4-Thread Shared-Memory Performance Audit and Stage-B Determinism Preflight}
\label{sec:stage14uae_4thread_performance}

Following the initial numerical parity verification in Stage~14U-AD, Stage~14U-AE establishes a quantitative performance benchmark for 4-thread shared-memory execution on the 14,483-element adaptive mesh and prepares the preflight qualification for the independent Stage-B determinism repeat.

\subsection{Node Allocation and Contention Discipline}
\label{subsec:stage14uae_contention}

A rigorous assessment of high-performance computing performance requires distinguishing unconstrained hardware potential from shared-resource multi-job execution. During the active evaluation window:
\begin{itemize}
  \item Serial reference Job \texttt{1409982.mmaster02} was allocated to compute node \texttt{mnode097/0} (1 core, 16\,GB memory);
  \item 4-thread candidate Job \texttt{1410006.mmaster02} was allocated to the same compute node \texttt{mnode097/1*4} (4 cores, 16\,GB memory).
\end{itemize}
Because both jobs executed concurrently on the same physical host, they shared the underlying memory bus bandwidth, L3 cache hierarchy, and node I/O channels. Consequently, the performance metrics reported herein are formally categorized under the epistemic classification:
\begin{quote}
  \textbf{Governing Performance Verdict:} \texttt{PERFORMANCE\_COMPARISON\_CONTENDED\_\_DESCRIPTIVE\_ONLY}
\end{quote}
This classification explicitly acknowledges that the measured speedup reflects real-world cluster operating throughput under concurrent multi-job conditions rather than idealized, uncontended single-user performance.

\begin{figure}[t!]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{fig_mode1_stage14uae_4thread_performance.pdf}

  \label{fig:stage14uae_4thread_performance}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Quantitative Throughput and Scaling Analysis}
\label{subsec:stage14uae_throughput}

Performance metrics were evaluated over the pre-peak Step~1 linear-elastic loading ramp ($u \in [0, 0.0050]\,\mathrm{mm}$, 2,000 target increments) on the 14,483-element adaptive mesh (Table~\ref{tab:stage14uae_performance_summary}):
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Walltime per Increment:} The serial 1-CPU solver required an average of $3.521\,\mathrm{s/inc}$ ($7,042\,\mathrm{s}$ for 2,000 increments), corresponding to a throughput of $1,022.4\,\mathrm{incs/hr}$. In comparison, the 4-thread shared-memory solver achieved $1.524\,\mathrm{s/inc}$ ($2,362.2\,\mathrm{incs/hr}$).
  \item \textbf{Measured Speedup and Efficiency:} The 4-thread configuration yielded a measured speedup:
  \begin{equation}
    S_4 = \frac{T_{\text{serial}}}{T_{\text{4-thread}}} = \frac{3.521\,\mathrm{s}}{1.524\,\mathrm{s}} = 2.310\times
  \end{equation}
  with a parallel scaling efficiency:
  \begin{equation}
    E_4 = \frac{S_4}{4} = \frac{2.310}{4} = 57.76\%
  \end{equation}
  \item \textbf{Walltime Savings:} Over the complete 2,000-increment elastic ramp of Step~1, 4-thread execution reduces walltime from $117.4\,\mathrm{min}$ ($1.96\,\mathrm{hr}$) to $50.8\,\mathrm{min}$ ($0.85\,\mathrm{hr}$), achieving an absolute walltime reduction of $66.6\,\mathrm{min}$ ($56.7\%$).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Stage-B Determinism Repeat Preflight}
\label{subsec:stage14uae_stage_b_preflight}

To satisfy the multi-threading governance protocol, parallel qualification cannot rely on a single Stage-A run. Package \texttt{27\_stage14\_adaptive\_candidate\_14k\_4thread\_stage\_b} was prepared to execute an independent determinism repeat under identical conditions:
\begin{itemize}
  \item Identical input deck SHA-256 (\texttt{26D873FB...});
  \item Identical Fortran source SHA-256 (\texttt{CE8D5EDC...});
  \item Identical runtime configuration ($1\text{ process} \times 4\text{ threads}$, \texttt{mp\_mode=threads}, $16\,\text{GB}$ memory);
  \item Distinct job name \texttt{PK\_M1\_14K\_4T\_STAGE\_B} to prevent file collisions.
\end{itemize}
The Abaqus Datacheck for Package~27 was executed on the cluster and passed with Exit Code~0 (zero errors, zero warnings). In accordance with the governance protocol, Package~27 is assigned the status \texttt{4THREAD\_STAGEB\_REPEAT\_VALIDATED\_\_WAITING\_FOR\_STAGEA\_TERMINAL\_PASS}. Its submission is held until Stage-A Job \texttt{1410006.mmaster02} terminates and completes its post-peak parity audit.

\paragraph{Physical Mechanism of Stagnation.}
The diagnostic telemetry reveals two key numerical facts:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{DOF-3 Specificity:} The largest correction occurs strictly on \textbf{Degree of Freedom 3 (the phase-field scalar $d$)} at severed ligament nodes (Node 13628 and Node 6479).
  \item \textbf{Correction Plateau in the Broken Tail:} Because the specimen is fully severed ($x_{\mathrm{tip}} = 0.9985\,\mathrm{mm}$, load drop $99.76\%$, $k=10^{-7}$), the mechanical resistance has degraded entirely. In this severed state, tiny localized phase-field oscillations ($\Delta d \approx 2.61\times 10^{-6}$) in essentially unloaded nodes fail the standard displacement correction check $\Delta u / \Delta u_{\text{inc}}$, even while the scaled residual force is minute ($1.94\times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{kN}$).
\end{enumerate}

  \item \textbf{Temporal Convergence Package 26 Status:} \texttt{PACKAGE26\_SUBMISSION\_BLOCKED\_\_SERIAL\_FAILED\_BEFORE\_0\_010000MM}
  \item \textbf{Parallel 4-Thread Status:} Job \texttt{1410006.mmaster02} remains actively solving in Step~2 with bitwise parity (\texttt{THREAD\_PARITY\_PASS\_OVER\_REACHED\_RANGE}), with Package~27 held pending its terminal qualification.
\end{itemize}

\section{Stage 14U-AG: Temporal-Refinement Diagnostic Submission, Attempt Telemetry Reconstruction, and Phase-Field Newton-Stagnation Root-Cause Audit}
\label{sec:stage14uag_temporal_diagnostic_and_stagnation_audit}

Following the historical-failure-crossing refailure of the serial baseline completion solve (Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02}) at $u = 0.007889\,\mathrm{mm}$ under extended cutback controls ($I_A=10$), Stage~14U-AG was initiated to execute two parallel investigation tracks:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{$2\times$ Temporal-Refinement Diagnostic Submission:} The pre-validated Package~26 candidate was released and submitted to the HPC cluster as Job~\texttt{1410027.mmaster02} to test whether halving the physical load incrementation ($\Delta u_1 = 1.25\,\mathrm{nm}, \Delta u_2 = 0.50\,\mathrm{nm}$) alters or eliminates the post-fracture Newton convergence stagnation while preserving all spatial and physical parameters.
  \item \textbf{Phase-Field Newton-Stagnation Root-Cause Audit:} Exhaustive solver telemetry reconstruction, physical coordinate mapping of stagnating nodes, authoritative Fortran subroutine equation auditing, and an offline finite-difference Jacobian verification harness were executed to establish the definitive mathematical root cause of solver stagnation.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Abaqus Incrementation Semantics and Increment 2890 Attempt Telemetry}
\label{subsec:stage14uag_attempt_telemetry}

An audit of the Abaqus 2023 nonlinear solver parameterization confirms that the keyword directive \texttt{*CONTROLS, PARAMETERS=TIME INCREMENTATION} controls cutback behavior via the eighth entry on the first parameter data line ($I_A$, default $I_A=5$). In Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02}, specifying $I_A=10$ expanded the allowable cutback attempts from 5 to 10 and allowed time step reduction down to the user-specified minimum $\Delta t_{\min} = 1.0\times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{s}$.

Table~\ref{tab:stage14uag_attempt_sequence} documents the complete attempt progression reconstructed from the solver execution message log (\texttt{.msg}) at Step~2 Increment~2890.

The attempt telemetry reveals a strict mathematical stagnation plateau:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Correction Plateau Invariance:} Across Attempts 7 through 10, the maximum nodal displacement correction remains \textbf{bitwise invariant at $\Delta d = 2.611\times 10^{-6}$} at Node~13628 (Degree of Freedom 3).
  \item \textbf{Residual Force Invariance:} The scaled residual force vector simultaneously plateaus at $R = 1.942\times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{kN}$ ($1.942\,\mu\mathrm{N}$) at Node~6479 (Degree of Freedom 3).
  \item \textbf{Equilibrium Acceptance vs.\ Correction Rejection:} In Abaqus/Standard, the residual force tolerance check evaluates $R_{\max} / \tilde{q} \le 0.005$, where $\tilde{q}$ is the overall time-averaged flux/force. At $u = 0.007889\,\mathrm{mm}$, the residual $R = 1.942\times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{kN}$ easily satisfies the force criterion ($R / \tilde{q} \approx 4.65\times 10^{-6} \ll 0.005$). However, the secondary displacement correction check requires $c_{\max} \le 0.01 \Delta u_{\mathrm{inc}}$. Because the time increment $\Delta t$ was progressively reduced down to $1.0\times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{s}$, the increment displacement shrank to $\Delta u_{\mathrm{inc}} = 5.0\times 10^{-12}\,\mathrm{mm}$, causing the ratio $c_{\max} / \Delta u_{\mathrm{inc}} \approx 5.2\times 10^5$ to diverge by over seven orders of magnitude above the convergence tolerance.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Physical Coordinate Mapping of Stagnating Nodes}
\label{subsec:stage14uag_spatial_mapping}

To identify whether stagnation originates at the propagating crack tip or in the severed crack wake, the spatial coordinates and damage states of the critical nodes were extracted from the converged solution database at Step~2 Increment~2889 ($u = 0.007889\,\mathrm{mm}$):
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Node 13628 (Maximum Correction):} $(x = 0.561986\,\mathrm{mm}, y = 0.496392\,\mathrm{mm})$, located $0.0620\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($8.27 l_0$) ahead of the initial notch root ($a_0 = 0.50\,\mathrm{mm}$), with damage $d = 0.9987$;
  \item \textbf{Node 6479 (Maximum Residual):} $(x = 0.563913\,\mathrm{mm}, y = 0.495441\,\mathrm{mm})$, located $0.0639\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($8.52 l_0$) ahead of the initial notch root, with damage $d = 0.9991$.
\end{itemize}
Both nodes lie strictly in the \textbf{fully severed crack wake} ($x \approx 0.56\,\mathrm{mm}$), far behind the macroscopically severed crack tip ($x_{\mathrm{tip}} = 0.9985\,\mathrm{mm}$). At these nodes, the material has degraded completely ($\sigma \approx 0, k=10^{-7}$), and elastic energy storage has collapsed. Consequently, Newton stagnation is not an active fracture initiation or propagation phenomenon, but a localized numerical ill-conditioning in the fully severed wake.

\subsection{Authoritative Fortran UEL Formulation and Jacobian Consistency Proof}
\label{subsec:stage14uag_jacobian_proof}

To determine whether the correction plateau arises from a coding error in the user element subroutine, an analytical audit of \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for} was conducted for the phase-field degree of freedom (Layer 1, U1/U3 elements). In the staggered/monolithic formulation, the element residual vector for the scalar phase field $d$ is:
\begin{equation}
  \mathbf{R}_d = \int_\Omega \left[ \left( \frac{G_c}{l_0} d - 2 (1-d) \mathcal{H} \right) \mathbf{N}^T + G_c l_0 \mathbf{B}^T \nabla d \right] \mathrm{d}\Omega
  \label{eq:uel_phase_residual}
\end{equation}
where $\mathbf{N}$ is the element shape function vector, $\mathbf{B} = \nabla \mathbf{N}$ is the shape function gradient matrix, and $\mathcal{H}$ is the crack history variable. The exact consistent tangent stiffness matrix (Jacobian) is:
\begin{equation}
  \mathbf{K}_d = \frac{\partial \mathbf{R}_d}{\partial \mathbf{d}} = \int_\Omega \left[ \left( \frac{G_c}{l_0} + 2 \mathcal{H} \right) \mathbf{N}^T \mathbf{N} + G_c l_0 \mathbf{B}^T \mathbf{B} \right] \mathrm{d}\Omega
  \label{eq:uel_phase_jacobian}
\end{equation}
Because $G_c > 0$, $l_0 > 0$, and $\mathcal{H} \ge 0$, the coefficient matrix $(G_c/l_0 + 2\mathcal{H}) > 0$ remains strictly positive for all $d \in [0, 1]$, proving that $\mathbf{K}_d$ is symmetric and strictly positive-definite even in the fully damaged limit $d \to 1$.

To verify implementation exactness, an offline numerical derivative test was executed on element $e = 7569$ (adjacent to Node~13628). The directional derivative of the residual vector was computed via central finite differences:
\begin{equation}
  \Delta \mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{FD}} = \frac{\mathbf{R}_d(\mathbf{d}_0 + \epsilon \delta\mathbf{d}) - \mathbf{R}_d(\mathbf{d}_0)}{\epsilon}
\end{equation}
and compared against the analytical tangent mapping $\mathbf{K}_d \delta\mathbf{d}$. As documented in Table~\ref{tab:stage14uag_jacobian_verification} and Figure~\ref{fig:stage14uag_jacobian_and_stagnation}(b), the relative discrepancy evaluates to $\mathbf{1.3622\times 10^{-14}}$ at $\epsilon = 10^{-2}$, matching double-precision machine epsilon ($2.22\times 10^{-16}$). This establishes the verdict \texttt{ZERO\_TANGENT\_INCONSISTENCY\_PROVEN}.

\subsection{Epistemic Hypothesis Classification and Root-Cause Verdict}
\label{subsec:stage14uag_hypotheses}

Four candidate hypotheses for solver stagnation were evaluated against numerical evidence:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Tangent Inconsistency in Phase UEL:} \texttt{NUMERICALLY\_VERIFIED (RULED\_OUT)}. The analytical Jacobian matches the numerical directional derivative within $1.36\times 10^{-14}$.
  \item \textbf{History Field Discontinuity:} \texttt{SOURCE\_VERIFIED (RULED\_OUT)}. In the wake, the strain history variable $\mathcal{H}$ has saturated and remains constant ($\Delta \mathcal{H} = 0$), introducing no non-smoothness.
  \item \textbf{Phase Saturation ($d \to 1$):} \texttt{SOURCE\_VERIFIED (OBSERVED\_STATE)}. At $d \approx 0.999$, the material stiffness matrix remains strictly positive-definite with zero negative eigenvalues.
  \item \textbf{Convergence Metric Mismatch Under Extreme Post-Fracture Softening:} \texttt{NUMERICALLY\_VERIFIED (PRIMARY\_ROOT\_CAUSE)}. Under $99.76\%$ load drop ($k = 10^{-7}$), mechanical force increments vanish ($\Delta u_{\mathrm{inc}} \to 0$), causing the relative displacement correction check $c_{\max} / \Delta u_{\mathrm{inc}}$ to fail despite force equilibrium residuals being satisfied by over $1000\times$.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Stage 14U-AH: Abaqus Nonlinear Convergence Criteria Reconstruction, Epistemic Classification, and Minimum-Control-Candidate Preflight}
\label{sec:stage14uah_convergence_reconstruction}

Following the completion run of Job \texttt{1409982.mmaster02} (Stage 14U-AF), which achieved a $99.76\%$ post-peak load drop before terminating at Step~2 Increment~2890 ($u = 0.007888\,\mathrm{mm}$) after 10 time cutbacks ($\Delta t = 2.0\times 10^{-4}\,\mathrm{s} \to 1.0\times 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{s}$), Stage~14U-AH executes a rigorous, multi-faceted investigation into the exact numerical mechanics governing solver termination. This section details:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item An authoritative primary documentation audit of Abaqus/Standard 2023 nonlinear solution controls and convergence criteria;
  \item A complete iteration-by-iteration numerical reconstruction of all 10 cutback attempts of Increment~2890;
  \item Formal epistemic classification of the four core governing hypotheses;
  \item A Fortran source audit verifying the absence of artificial phase-field clipping; and
  \item The definition, preflight implementation, and cluster Datacheck verification of minimal solver-control candidate Package~28 (\texttt{models/pandey\_kumar\_mode1/28\_stage14\_convergence\_control\_candidate}).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Abaqus 2023 Nonlinear Solution Controls Formulation}
\label{subsec:stage14uah_abaqus_formulation}

In the default Abaqus/Standard solver formulation (\textit{Abaqus 2023 Primary Documentation §7.2.2: Convergence Criteria for Nonlinear Problems}), global equilibrium iterations for each active degree-of-freedom field $\alpha$ are governed by two independent criteria:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Residual Force Equilibrium Test:}
  \begin{equation}
    R_{\max}^\alpha \le R_n^\alpha \tilde{q}^\alpha
    \label{eq:abaqus_residual_criterion}
  \end{equation}
  where $R_{\max}^\alpha$ is the largest nodal residual force component, $\tilde{q}^\alpha$ is the time-average flux normalizer computed dynamically as the running average of internal force norms across all converged increments, and $R_n^\alpha$ is the convergence tolerance (default $R_n = 0.005 = 0.5\%$).
  \item \textbf{Solution Correction Test:}
  \begin{equation}
    c_{\max}^\alpha \le C_n^\alpha \Delta u_{\max}^\alpha
    \label{eq:abaqus_correction_criterion}
  \end{equation}
  where $c_{\max}^\alpha$ is the maximum nodal solution correction vector component in the current iteration, $\Delta u_{\max}^\alpha$ is the maximum incremental change of the field over the current increment, and $C_n^\alpha$ is the correction tolerance (default $C_n = 0.01 = 1.0\%$).
\end{enumerate}

In the 3-layer UEL user element formulation (\texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for}), degrees of freedom 1, 2, and 3 are mapped under the primary displacement field (\texttt{FIELD=DISPLACEMENT}). Consequently, Abaqus evaluates both the nodal mechanical displacement corrections $(\delta u_x, \delta u_y)$ and the nodal phase-field corrections $\delta d$ against the single global incremental displacement metric $\Delta u_{\max}$.

When the Mode-I crack severs the specimen across its entire ligament ($d \approx 0.999$, residual stiffness factor $k = 10^{-7}$), the mechanical load drops by $99.76\%$, causing the elastic body to unload into near-rigid macroscopic motion. Under subsequent automatic time cutbacks, the incremental displacement shrinks directly with time step size ($\Delta u_{\max} \propto \Delta t \to 0$). However, in the fully severed crack wake, minute variational adjustments and numerical roundoff maintain a microscopic phase update plateau of $c_{\max} = \delta d \approx 2.611\times 10^{-6}$ at Node~13628 (DOF~3). 

Because the correction tolerance scales down as $C_n \Delta u_{\max} \propto \Delta t$, the rejection ratio explodes from $22.2\times$ at $\Delta t = 2\times 10^{-4}\,\mathrm{s}$ to over $5.2\times 10^6\times$ at $\Delta t = 10^{-9}\,\mathrm{s}$, rendering time cutbacks mathematically futile despite the residual equilibrium criterion being satisfied by over $1000\times$ margin ($R_{\max} / (R_n \tilde{q}) \approx 0.000929$).

\subsection{Phase-Field Boundlessness Audit in User Subroutine}
\label{subsec:stage14uah_fortran_audit}

A source code audit of \texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for} confirmed that no artificial projection or numerical clipping operators (such as \texttt{min(max(d, 0.0), 1.0)}) exist in the phase-field UEL formulation. 

It is important to distinguish local algebraic solutions from the global finite-element system. The algebraic relation $d = \frac{2\mathcal{H}}{G_c/l_0 + 2\mathcal{H}}$ represents the local, homogeneous minimizer in the absence of spatial gradients. Because the implemented AT2 formulation incorporates the spatial gradient regularization $\frac{1}{2} G_c l_0 |\nabla d|^2$, the local formula cannot by itself prove a global nodal bound for the actual discretized boundary-value problem. The verified fact is that the Fortran source applies no artificial numerical clipping, and at the controlling wake node (Node~13628), the physical damage evaluates naturally to $d = 0.9987 \in [0, 1)$.

\subsection{Package 28 Specification and Cluster Datacheck Qualification}
\label{subsec:stage14uah_package28_qualification}

To test whether relaxing the non-physical displacement correction tolerance on the severed crack wake relieves post-fracture Newton stagnation, candidate \textbf{Package~28} (\texttt{models/pandey\_kumar\_mode1/28\_stage14\_convergence\_control\_candidate}) was constructed:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Single Non-Invasive Modification:} In Step~2, displacement field controls are appended:
  \begin{verbatim}
*CONTROLS, PARAMETERS=FIELD, FIELD=DISPLACEMENT
0.005, 0.50
  \end{verbatim}
  specifying $R_n = 0.005$ (standard $0.5\%$ equilibrium tolerance) and $C_n = 0.50$ ($50\%$ correction tolerance).
  \item \textbf{Derivation of $50\times$ Relaxation:} In Attempt~1 of Increment~2890, the observed solution correction was $c_{\max} = 2.611\times 10^{-6}$ against $\Delta u_{\max} = 1.175\times 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{mm}$ (ratio $c_{\max}/\Delta u_{\max} = 0.222$). Under default $C_n = 0.01$, tolerance is $1.175\times 10^{-7}\,\mathrm{mm}$ ($22.2\times$ excess). Relaxing $C_n$ to $0.50$ ($50\times$ default) scales the tolerance to $5.875\times 10^{-6}\,\mathrm{mm}$, yielding $c_{\max}/\text{Tol} = 0.444 < 1.0$. This parameter is an empirical diagnostic candidate and is not designated as a validated production standard.
  \item \textbf{Strict Physical Invariance:} All mesh nodes (14,456), base elements (14,483), layered elements (43,449), material properties ($E=210\,\mathrm{GPa}$, $\nu=0.3$, $G_c=0.0027\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$, $l_0=0.0075\,\mathrm{mm}$, $k=10^{-7}$), and Fortran source (\texttt{f42\_mixed\_uel.for}) remain completely unchanged.
  \item \textbf{Cluster Datacheck Execution:} Executed directly on the HPC cluster (\texttt{run\_datacheck\_direct.sh}) achieving \textbf{Exit Code 0} with 0 errors and 0 warnings.
  \item \textbf{Submission Gate Status:} Formally designated as:
  \begin{equation}
    \text{Status: } \mathbf{CONVERGENCE\_CRITERION\_CANDIDATE\_VALIDATED\_\_TEMPORAL\_DIAGNOSTIC\_PENDING}
  \end{equation}
  Package~28 remains strictly on hold and un-submitted while active diagnostic Job \texttt{1410027.mmaster02} is executing.
\end{itemize}

\section{Stage 14U-AI: Epistemic Claim Corrections, 4-Thread Near-Failure Parity Audit, and Governed Determinism Policy}
\label{sec:stage14uai_claim_corrections_and_parity}

In Stage 14U-AI, the scientific record and coordination governance were audited to enforce strict epistemic precision, while tracking the active 4-thread Stage-A solve (Job \texttt{1410006.mmaster02}) and $2\times$ temporal diagnostic solve (Job \texttt{1410027.mmaster02}).

\subsection{Epistemic Claim Corrections and Governance Rules}
\label{subsec:stage14uai_epistemic_corrections}

To ensure complete defensibility without unsupported extrapolations:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Phase-Field Nodal Bounds:} The local no-gradient algebraic expression $d(\mathcal{H})$ was decoupled from global finite-element bound claims. Global bounds in the presence of $\frac{1}{2} G_c l_0 |\nabla d|^2$ require weak-form maximum principle analysis; the source audit confirms strictly the absence of explicit clipping in Fortran.
  \item \textbf{Matrix Conditioning vs Normalization Sensitivity:} The classification of solver failure was downgraded from \texttt{POST\_FRACTURE\_ILL\_CONDITIONING} to \texttt{NOT\_ESTABLISHED}, and replaced with the verified mechanism \texttt{POST\_FRACTURE\_CONVERGENCE\_NORMALIZATION\_SENSITIVITY\_VERIFIED}.
  \item \textbf{History-Field Non-Smoothness Scope:} The exclusion of history discontinuities was explicitly scoped to the controlling wake node (Node~13628, $\dot{\mathcal{H}} = 0$) rather than asserted globally.
  \item \textbf{Package 28 $C_n = 0.50$ Scope:} The $50\times$ tolerance relaxation was mathematically derived from Attempt~1 telemetry and classified strictly as an un-submitted candidate.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Exact Stagnation Plateau:} In both runs, Attempts 7 through 10 plateau at $c_{\max} = 2.611\times 10^{-6}\,\mathrm{mm}$ on Degree of Freedom 3 (phase-field $d$) at Node~13628.
  \item \textbf{Residual Force Equilibrium:} Residual force equilibrium is continuously satisfied ($R_{\max} / (R_n \tilde{q}) = 0.000929 \ll 1.0$), proving that termination is strictly driven by the displacement correction normalization check under vanishing incremental displacement ($\Delta u_{\max} \propto \Delta t \to 0$).
  \item \textbf{Thread Determinism Under Softening:} Shared-memory multithreading introduces zero numerical perturbation, race conditions, or bifurcation in the phase-field Newton iterations even in the extreme post-peak softening regime ($99.76\%$ load drop).
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Parallel Speedup and Scaling Efficiency}
\label{subsec:stage14uaj_parallel_scaling}

As illustrated in Figure~\ref{fig:stage14uaj_scaling_and_attempts}(a), 4-thread shared-memory execution achieved substantial performance gains:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Total Walltime Reduction:} $17{,}609\,\mathrm{s} \to 7{,}627\,\mathrm{s}$ ($2.77\,\mathrm{hrs}$ saved per full fracture simulation);
  \item \textbf{Measured Parallel Speedup:} $S_4 = 17{,}609 / 7{,}627 = \mathbf{2.31\times}$;
  \item \textbf{Parallel Efficiency:} $E_4 = S_4 / 4 = \mathbf{57.7\%}$ under contended compute node memory bus sharing on \texttt{mnode097};
  \item \textbf{Throughput Rate:} Step-1 throughput increased from $1{,}022.4\,\mathrm{incs/hr}$ in serial to $2{,}362.2\,\mathrm{incs/hr}$ in 4-thread mode.
\end{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Energy Partitioning Parity:} $|\Delta E_{\mathrm{elas}}|_{\max} = 0.000000\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $|\Delta E_{\mathrm{frac}}|_{\max} = 0.000000\,\mathrm{mJ}$, $|\Delta W_{\mathrm{ext}}|_{\max} = 0.000000\,\mathrm{mJ}$, and $|\Delta \varepsilon_{\mathrm{book}}|_{\max} = 0.000000\%$;
  \item \textbf{Canonical Structural Stiffness Invariance:} Ordinary least-squares regression across the $N=400$ increments ($u \in [0.0000025, 0.0010000]\,\mathrm{mm}$) reproduces the canonical initial structural stiffness bitwise to 8 decimal places:
  \begin{equation}
    K_{0,\text{Stage-B}} = 137.90955785\,\mathrm{kN/mm} \quad (R^2 = 0.99999960, \text{intercept } 4.471205\times 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{kN})

  matching the fixed uniform reference ($137.945520\,\mathrm{kN/mm}$) within $-0.0261\%$ (\texttt{STABLE});
  \item \textbf{Governing Classification:} Formally designated as \texttt{STAGE\_B\_DETERMINISM\_PARITY\_PASS\_OVER\_REACHED\_RANGE}.
\end{itemize}

\section{Stage 14U-AL: Automated Evaluator Freeze, Decision Protocols, and 8-Thread Scaling Roadmap}
\label{sec:stage14ual_evaluators_and_decision_protocols}

Building upon the Stage-B determinism repeat initialization and temporal checkpoint in Stage~14U-AK, Stage~14U-AL locks and validates the complete, turnkey automated evaluation infrastructure, establishes formal pre-declared decision protocols and schemas, prepares the Package~29 8-thread Stage-A twin template, and audits live dual-solver telemetry on compute node \texttt{mnode097}.

\subsection{Automated Evaluator Architecture and Mathematical Rigor}
\label{subsec:stage14ual_evaluator_architecture}

Two specialized, turnkey post-processing evaluators have been authored and verified within the active package structures:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Evaluator A (\texttt{evaluate\_stage14ual\_4thread\_determinism.py}):} Deployed in Package~27 (\texttt{models/pandey\_kumar\_mode1/27\_stage14\_adaptive\_candidate\_14k\_4thread\_stage\_b/}), this tool executes automated, multi-tier comparison between 4-Thread Stage-B (Job~\texttt{1410029.mmaster02}), 4-Thread Stage-A (Job~\texttt{1410006.mmaster02}), and Serial Reference (Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02}). The evaluator tracks:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item Pointwise increment-by-increment reaction force, strain energy, fracture functional, and external work parity;
    \item Canonical linear-elastic structural stiffness $K_0$ ($N=400$ increments, $u \le 0.0010\,\mathrm{mm}$) via ordinary least squares;
    \item Ten pre-declared matched actual reference-point displacement states:
    \begin{equation}
      u \in \{0.0010, 0.0030, 0.0050, 0.005733, 0.005857, 0.0060, 0.0065, 0.0070, 0.007889\}\,\mathrm{mm}
    \end{equation}
    plus any subsequent post-fracture states reached by the solver;
    \item Peak capacity ($F_{\max}, u_{\mathrm{peak}}$) and terminal softening characteristics;
    \item Increment~2890 cutback attempt sequence ($c_{\max}, R_{\max}$, controlling wake node/DOF, $\Delta t$, iterations, cutbacks).
  \end{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Evaluator B (\texttt{evaluate\_stage14ual\_temporal\_refinement.py}):} Deployed in Package~26 (\texttt{models/pandey\_kumar\_mode1/26\_stage14\_temporal\_refined\_candidate\_2x/}), this tool automates the comparison between the $2\times$ temporal refinement diagnostic (Job~\texttt{1410027.mmaster02}) and the baseline solve (Job~\texttt{1409982.mmaster02}). The tool evaluates energy partitioning dynamics ($E_{\mathrm{elas}}/W_{\mathrm{ext}}, E_{\mathrm{frac}}/W_{\mathrm{ext}}$), canonical stiffness $K_0$ ($N=800$ temporal increments or $N=400$ interpolated baseline grid), peak load evolution, and automatically classifies the terminal run into one of three pre-declared decision branches at $u = 0.007889\,\mathrm{mm}$.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Epistemic Separation and Governance Discipline}
\label{subsec:stage14ual_epistemic_governance}

Strict epistemic governance is enforced across all evaluation documents:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Decoupling of Determinism vs.\ Representation Accuracy:} The evaluation between Stage-A and Stage-B repeats assesses \textbf{parallel numerical determinism across independent scheduler allocations}. In contrast, comparison against the fixed uniform reference ($15,192$ elements) assesses \textbf{structural compliance restoration and mesh representation fidelity}. These two scientific evaluations must never be conflated.
  \item \textbf{Governed Nomenclature:} The phase-field regularized fracture energy $E_{\mathrm{frac}}$ is strictly designated as the \textbf{"implemented phase-field crack-surface/fracture functional"}.
  \item \textbf{Failure Mechanism Formulation:} Post-fracture termination under residual softening is classified as \textbf{\texttt{POST\_FRACTURE\_CONVERGENCE\_NORMALIZATION\_SENSITIVITY\_VERIFIED}}, while matrix ill-conditioning remains classified as \textbf{\texttt{NOT\_ESTABLISHED}} pending formal condition number estimation.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Predeclared Decision Branches at Historical Failure Point}
\label{subsec:stage14ual_decision_branches}

To eliminate post-hoc evaluation bias, three mutually exclusive decision branches are formally pre-declared for the $2\times$ temporal diagnostic at $u = 0.007889\,\mathrm{mm}$ (Table~\ref{tab:stage14ual_decision_branches}):
